P7102:
P7103: ### 15.3 The day-close leg, written against bound variables
P7104:
P7105: **15.3.1 Marks, cap, initialiser, and the life boolean.** THE DESIGN REQUIREMENT, restated rather than carried: marks derive from the evaluated bar's own date with no pilot bound, and the 32-entry cap does not limit the verdict. THE SOURCE STILL DOES NEITHER, and that is the open defect rather than a resolution of it - the current marks are built only between `SRJ_PILOT_FROM` and `SRJ_PILOT_TO` (EA 10882-10883) in a loop capped at 32 entries (EA 11005-11006, closing EA 11021-11022), so the June window carries NO day marks and `vDAY` cannot fire there. CONSEQUENCE: the DAY_CLOSE branch of 15.4.7 is NOT gradeable on any June fixture until the day-mark pilot window is closed, and 15.6 keeps that defect separate; the lazy initialiser is displaced because the test requires its completion flag; the day and week-close life booleans migrate to the repaired derivation and are removed as day-close evidence entirely, because the source compares the mark strictly against the exit bar time while the verdict is inclusive, so that boolean cannot read true for its own day-close exit under either trigger.
P7106:
P7107: **15.3.2 The trigger, restated against the variable that actually holds it.** Bindings, all in EX-28: inside the managed-trade host, `barTime` is assigned from the bar index passed in and the host is invoked with the last closed bar; the price local is the open of the bar before that index, falling back to the evaluated bar's close; the day-close gate compares the mark at or before `barTime`. So with the evaluated bar's time in hand, the forming bar's open is `barTime + one period`, and the verdict bar is the FORMING bar, never the evaluated one.
P7108:
P7109: - PRIMARY FORM: the verdict fires when `barTime + one period <= mark < barTime + two periods` - that is, when the mark falls at or after the forming bar's open and before the next bar's open. The booked price is the existing price local on that pass, which is that forming bar's open. For a mark aligned to a bar open the left inequality is an equality, which is why the interval must be closed on the left. FILL CONTAINMENT, restated from the source term the previous fold omitted: the mark must also be at or after the fill, that is `fillBarTime <= mark`, exactly as the current source requires of the mark it compares at EA 12017. THE FILL TERM IS REDUNDANT IN THIS FORM AND IS SAID SO RATHER THAN RESTATED AS PROTECTION: the managed-trade host returns before any comparison when the evaluated bar predates the fill (`if(barTime < g_mtrade.fillBarTime) return;`, EA 11849), so inside this form the mark is already at or after the fill. The same-fill-bar mark is therefore NOT reachable in this form at all; it belongs to the catch-up of 15.3.4, which is the only path where it can occur.
P7110: - PERMITTED ALTERNATIVE: bind the comparison to the forming bar's own open time read from the current bar index instead, and state what then remains of the old test inside the closed-bar host.
P7111: - What is forbidden IN THE ON-TIME TRIGGER: using `mark <= barTime` as the firing test, which is exactly what the current source tests at EA 12017. The comparison itself is not banned and 15.3.4 prescribes it as part of the catch-up, so the prohibition is scoped to the on-time trigger and the catch-up is its sole licensed use; and any interval that COVERS THE EVALUATED BAR'S OWN SPAN - an interval whose left endpoint is at or before the evaluated bar's own time and whose right endpoint is at or inside that bar's own open edge. Endpoints taken at the forming bar's open and at the next bar's open are the REQUIRED form, not a forbidden one, and the census row T1 below carries the same reading. The previous wording, "any interval whose endpoints are measured against the evaluated bar's own time", forbade the very form printed above it and is withdrawn.
P7112:
P7113: The verdict row names the forming bar and prints both the verdict-bar open and the next-bar open.
P7114:
P7115: **15.3.3 Division of labour between the two hosts, and why a tick host exists.** The stated reason in v15 was wrong and is withdrawn. The closed-bar host's new-bar guard passes exactly on the first tick of each new bar, so timing alone does not require a second host; the real case is latency, because the pool refresh, the working-set load and the closed-bar evaluation run first inside that host. So: the closed-bar host books the verdict, and an optional check inserted ahead of the new-bar guard exists to act earlier than the other work on the same tick. RESOLVED, because a check that books nothing buys no latency and the previous fold left it advancing only a latch: that earlier check is a FULL evaluation, not a latch advance. It computes the same per-leg verdicts for the evaluated bar - SL, synchronized target, BREAK, HTF, DAY_CLOSE - under the same precedence line as the closed-bar host, and it books or closes on that tick. Its own opening-tick detection fires only on the first tick of the bar whose open equals the mark and never otherwise. ONE RULE, so the clauses cannot both apply: FIRST WRITER WINS, and the guard is PER INSTANCE AND PER EVALUATED BAR ACROSS EVERY LEG - SL, synchronized target, BREAK, HTF and DAY_CLOSE - not a day-close-only latch. That matters because the earlier check evaluates all five: with a day-close-only latch an early BREAK or SL close would be re-evaluated by the closed-bar host on the same tick, the second `MtCloseBrokerPosition` would return NOTHING-TO-CLOSE (EA 11808-11811), and the caller prints MTCLOSE_FAIL on a zero return (EA 12058-12059), so a single tick would produce a spurious failure row. With a per-instance per-evaluated-bar verdict latch the host stands down for the whole evaluation, and "at most one close call per tick" becomes true rather than unproven. The earlier check runs first inside its tick, sets the latch and acts; the closed-bar host reads that latch at the top of its own evaluation and stands down from the day-close leg when it is already set for that instance. The host never overwrites an early booking and the earlier check never double-acts, so one tick yields at most one close call. The once-per-instance latch is therefore a double-act guard, not the earlier check's only output. The latch is retired with the check if the check is dropped; it is not a load-bearing verdict of its own.
P7116:
P7117: **15.3.4 A visible late day-close catch-up.** An equality-only trigger can be missed entirely - by a data hole, an early return, or a skipped pass - and a missed day-close is the overnight hold that is the regression itself. So an inclusive catch-up is retained: if the mark is at or after the fill, at or before the evaluated bar, and no verdict has fired for the instance, the leg fires visibly as a LATE day-close row carrying its own reference, and the fail-soft in the price local is named in that row rather than substituting silently. The fill-containment term is stated in the catch-up as well as in the primary form, because without it the catch-up is the one path that could fire on a mark from BEFORE the fill bar on the first post-fill pass; the primary form cannot, because the host gate already forces the mark past the fill bar. The same-fill-bar mark remains eligible, inclusive as the source is.
P7118:
P7119: **15.3.5 The writer's firing tick.** The revision fires at the first tick of the first out-of-session bar, on the same terms as day-close; the sentence describing a one-bar lag described the current host and is withdrawn. On that pass the evaluated bar is the LAST IN-SESSION bar, so the extreme walk must include the evaluated bar itself or the session extreme can miss the final in-session bar - and the two baseline regression values police exactly that. Target-passed is re-scoped to three cases: a target already passed at the open, a modification overtaken by a touch while failed or pending, and residual in-flight lag. NEW PINS, stated because the firing tick reverses two of the current helper's pins and moves the walk start: (1) the out-of-session test is applied to the bar AFTER the evaluated one, because on the firing pass the evaluated bar is the last in-session bar - the current first-out-bar pin at EA 1902 and EA 1905 is superseded for this call only, and the forming-bar test reads `iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, 0)`, which the exclusive session end at EA 1885-1887 permits; (2) the walk start is `barShift`, not `barShift+1` as the current helper starts at EA 1911, so the evaluated bar is inside the walked run. The walk end and the admission-containment test at EA 1927 are unchanged. LATE RETARGET, and here the honest answer is the declared loss, not the extra mechanism: the first-out-bar pin tests the bar AFTER the evaluated one, which holds only on the firing pass where the evaluated bar is the last in-session bar, so on any later pass the pinned helper returns false and a LATE walk anchored the way the day-close catch-up is anchored cannot run. A catch-up re-anchored on the session close event is new selection machinery and is not invented here. The revision is therefore ONE-SHOT BY DECLARATION: a skipped pass loses that session's revision visibly, the miss is recorded in the retarget row as a declared loss rather than a silent one, and the acceptance requires that row whenever a session close passed with no revision recorded.
P7120:
P7121: ### 15.4 Q1 contract
P7122:
P7123: **15.4.1 Eligible sessions and revision sources.** LONDON and NYAM only, with the close event at or after the fill. THE TWO CONTAINMENT FORMS, stated separately because the earlier fold merged them into a sentence that cancelled itself: the ENTRY session's run keeps the source's admission-containment test at EA 1927, which requires the walked run to contain the trade's admission bar, because as written that test rejects every run not containing it; a LATER eligible session's run carries NO admission test and is bound only by close-at-or-after-the-fill eligibility and by the nearest rule of 15.4.3. Reading both runs as carrying EA 1927 would reject every later session and negate the YES printed in this same paragraph. The closed-session helper is hard-wired to the entry session at four sites, so the closing session becomes a parameter at all four; whether a later session's close may revise a still-open trade is answered by the pin rather than left silent. THE ANSWER, written out because the previous fold left the pin to speak for itself: YES. The pin reads "once a session is closed, a floating trade can retarget that new session high or low as the nearest valid target", so every eligible session closing after the fill may revise the still-open trade, and the two containment forms above apply per session rather than one admission test applied to every run. The swept mask is buffer-slot-indexed and does not apply to the own-bar walk; the buffer path keeps it. **The gapped POC is in scope on the evidence of SKILL line 32, spliced at 15.1** - it names a newer session close or a gapped POC as the dynamic revised target - and its detection predicate and its producer are now named, which the previous fold did not do: a gapped POC is the POC of an eligible closed session that the market has gapped THROUGH and now sits beyond, so that it remains a target AHEAD of price: for a LONG the session POC lies at or ABOVE the current bar's HIGH with that bar's open at or BELOW it, and for a SHORT the mirror, the session POC at or BELOW the current bar's LOW with that bar's open at or ABOVE it. The geometry is the source's own reclaimed-through shape at EA 2368 - `allowReclaim && o1 <= L && c0 >= o1` for a LONG, an open at or below the line - applied to a revision target rather than to a confirmation; the earlier fold wrote the LONG clause with that direction inverted, which placed the target behind price so that every firing of the detector landed in row 6 and could never book, and that wording is withdrawn. THE DETECTOR IS DECLARED UNSOURCED AND NOT GRADED, on measured evidence rather than on judgement: the POI line universe is exactly the twelve entries named at EA 93-104 - FOMC, Yearly, Quarterly, Monthly, Weekly and Daily POC and VWAP - and NONE of them is a London or NYAM session POC, so there is no buffer from which a session POC value could be read and no producer that writes one. The earlier fold named a predicate over a value that does not exist in this EA, and the seat check found it: for a LONG the clause reduced to "POC at or above the bar high" because the open clause is implied by open <= high, so it was not a gap test either. A session-POC buffer is new strategy surface and is NOT invented here. What remains in scope and printable is the closed-session branch alone, and this detector is declared NOT GRADED until a source exists - recorded as an open implementation obligation beside 15.4.6, not as a discharged ask. Its producer is the revision-detection writer named in 15.4.2. The ASIA and PM exclusion stands as a known gap with its journal census owed; his symmetric pin is untouched.
P7124:
P7125: **15.4.2 Who writes what, with the vocabulary separated.** A revision RECORD is created at detection: the retarget row prints unconditionally, the record goes PENDING_SYNC, and no target is written. The gapped-POC detector is declared UNSOURCED and NOT GRADED at 15.4.1 - the POI line universe at EA 93-104 holds no session POC, so there is no value for it to read - and it is therefore NOT a recorder here and no revision record is promised for it. The closed-session detector alone creates a revision RECORD on detection, and the booked target still arrives only through the sync helper success path. A BOOKED target is written only on confirmed synchronization. One mechanism books: the sync helper's success path. The one-shot touch election writes no revision, though it does write its own latch fields which migrate per instance; the nearest-target helper feeds only a recompute counter and is not a revision source, which is why the gapped POC needed a predicate of its own rather than the recompute.
P7126:
P7127: **15.4.3 Transition table, printed in full.** Sixteen rows. It is printed here rather than cited from an earlier section because this fold replaces section 15 entirely, and a claim of carry is not a carry.
P7128:
P7129: | # | State or event | Next | Binding notes |
P7130: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
P7131: | 1 | Fill in execute or tester | OPEN; identity registered after the confirmed fill; revision 0 equals the broker target, SYNCED | no instance on a failed entry; AND the sync helper's send path is no-send unless execute AND tester are both true, the identical conjunction the existing close helper uses at EA 11795, so alert-only can never issue a PositionModify |
P7132: | 2 | Eligible nearer closed-session target or gapped POC at or after the fill | revision n PENDING_SYNC; retarget row printed unconditionally; first attempt on the same pass | nearer means nearer than the NEAREST of the booked target and the pending revision, never nearer than the booked target alone, so a later session's extreme that is nearer than booked but looser than the pending revision cannot cancel n and install a looser target; a revision cancelled by a newer one is labelled SUPERSEDED, prints its own row, and if it was never confirmed emits exactly one EXIT-UNSYNCED row naming the superseding revision |
P7133: | 3 | Modify DONE, exact readback equal to the revision, SL unchanged | SYNCED(n) | one sync row per revision, never per bar |
P7134: | 4 | Retryable failure (TIMEOUT, CONNECTION, PRICE_CHANGED, TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, REQUOTE) | PENDING; at most three actual calls, one per M5 bar | market-closed is a visible deferral and spends no call |
P7135: | 5 | Permanent failure, unknown retcode, or the third failure | SYNC_FAILED(n); one EXIT-UNSYNCED row per revision | original SL and target intact; no market close |
P7136: | 6 | Target already passed at the open, or inside stops or freeze | no call; revision unaccepted | **when the refusal is really the stops level, the old target and stop stay in force and the position may stop out after the model reported a touch - that class grades FAIL and is named, not called settled** |
P7137: | 7 | NO_CHANGES | SKIP only when the readback equals the revision | a skip is not a call and spends no budget |
P7138: | 8 | Model touch while SYNCED | AWAIT_DEAL; MODEL_ONLY when no deal follows | the instance stays live |
P7139: | 9 | Model touch while PENDING or SYNC_FAILED | EXIT-UNSYNCED row and alert | the instance stays live and masks no other leg |
P7140: | 10 | Identity absent with a full-volume exit deal | RESOLVED; retired | **lifecycle only: a deal at the last-synchronized revision may retire the instance but does NOT pass acceptance when a newer revision was pending or failed, and a deal at a pending revision whose modify returned done but whose readback has not confirmed is its own named case** |
P7141: | 11 | BREAK | market close, at most three calls, one per eligible bar | **retries persist on the latched verdict, never on a per-bar recomputation, and the retry reference price is the original verdict price, not the retry bar's open** |
P7142: | 12 | HTF | non-executing; the instance stays live | HTF-off asserted at acceptance |
P7143: | 13 | DAY_CLOSE | as row 11 | price reference per 15.3.2; trigger and catch-up per 15.3.3 and 15.3.4 |
P7144: | 14 | Close pending | CLOSE_PENDING; still managed | same latch rule as row 11 |
P7145: | 15 | Close terminal failure | CLOSE_FAILED, visible, still managed | other legs keep evaluating |
P7146: | 16 | Identity absent with a history lookup | resolve the deal; else orphan published and still awaiting broker resolution | history-lookup retries bounded |
P7147:
P7148: Same-bar precedence: SL, synchronized target, BREAK, HTF, DAY_CLOSE. An unsynchronized touch never outranks another winning leg.
P7149:
P7150: **15.4.4 Registration, its counters, and the two O6 gates.** Registration moves after the confirmed fill in execute mode; the alert-only paper instance registers at the snapshot with its flag meaning "not yet model-closed", which is also that mode's concurrency predicate. The counters are dispositioned from EX-28: the trade sequence and the admission count are consumed at the snapshot together with the admission row, so a failed entry leaves a consumed sequence and an admission row with no instance - the sequence is therefore released back on any pre-fill abort and the admission row is annotated rather than left implying an instance. The record-replacement and reset block may not run against a live identity. EVERY READER OF A MIGRATED OR REMOVED COUNTER IS NAMED HERE rather than deferred elsewhere: TWO READER LISTS, because the earlier fold ran them together: the RUN-GLOBAL `uj_tradeSeqNext` is written at EA 10731 and read at exactly one place, the memo pass at EA 10700; the PER-INSTANCE FIELD `g_mtrade.uj_tradeSeq`, assigned from that global at EA 10731, is what the admission row at EA 10737, the touch row at EA 11635, both re-elect rows at EA 11684 and EA 11691, and the retarget and no-retarget rows at EA 11921 and EA 11923 actually print. Releasing the global therefore changes EA 10700, while migrating the field changes those six rows, and the two lists are kept apart so a migration cannot miss the split. `uj_admitCount` is written at EA 10732 and read by the pool-service row at EA 12227; the migrated recompute counter `g_n1_tpRecomputeSupp` is read in the verdict tail at EA 12032. Releasing the sequence on a pre-fill abort changes what EA 10700 and EA 12227 print, so both are named as affected rows. The N1 family is disposed separately in 15.5.4 and its own census, and the two lists do not overlap.
P7151:
P7152: **O6 has TWO gates and both are required.** The pre-seed gate sits at the IDLE boundary and the pre-side-effect gate sits ahead of the signal effects and the record replacement. Both use local session magic, include older same-session positions, print a named block row, and consume no sequence, admission, signal, refused-candidate or session use. The pre-seed gate is the NEW gate and the ordering claim attaches to it; the legacy execute-path position check remains the execute-mode equivalent. On the release bar the block must precede the fresh seed, which is what makes the June 11 ordering work: a still-open June 5 position blocks the June 11 candidate before any state is consumed.
P7153:
P7154: **15.4.5 Flag migration, with the producers named.** The gates to migrate are the two target-dependent gates, the verdict write, and the local close path; the producers to migrate are the touch local and both of its set sites, the recompute counter and the verdict write, all spliced at EX-30. An unsynchronized touch never outranks another winning leg.
P7155:
P7156: **15.4.6 References and posture.** No numeric retcode, overload, history call, close reason, hedging or netting posture, or asynchronous setting binds from memory; every one is verified against the current reference and the account before encoding. The retryable classes and the market-closed no-call deferral are proposed classifications until that check.
P7157:
P7158: **15.4.7 Q1 acceptance, printed here because this fold replaces section 15 entirely and a claim of carry is not a carry.** The two branches are non-combinable. BRANCH TP, on two separate positions with no combinable fixture: June 5 New York target 160.298 volume 0.41 same PID; June 5 London target 159.908 volume 6.74 same PID. For each, prove target revision, then a successful accepted modification, then an exact broker TP readback, then an unchanged SL, then a full-volume same-PID DEAL_ENTRY_OUT with DEAL_REASON_TP at the operative revised target, with no old TP and no stop first; a deal at a superseded revision or at the original price fails. Carry every later eligible closed-session revision and every supersession event through the diff, and print for each the closed-session value it carried, the extreme walk's first and last bar, and the fact that the touch evaluation for the last in-session bar used the OLD booked target while the revision was pending. The two baseline values 159.908 and 160.298 police the re-based walk of 15.3.5 and must reproduce. Start the diff at June 5 London 12:05 and run it through June 11 and journal end, including full attempt, retcode, readback, identity, volume and reason rows. BRANCH DAY_CLOSE, on a SEPARATE FIXTURE, because the TP branch consumes that position: a live managed position with no eligible session target at its daily mark, the preserved 4 September EURUSD scenario as model-level history, and an actual same-PID full-volume broker close at the 23:55 verdict-day reference. That branch is reported in two cases, the on-time case and the LATE catch-up case, separately, with first-tick latency recorded as class two in both. The executable fill price is recorded as its own observation against the first tick of the bar and is never generalised into a market-fill rule for other legs. THREE ADDITIONS, each from a named seat condition. (1) A LATE day-close fixture is required as its own case, so the catch-up path is exercised rather than described. (2) A gapped-POC fixture is required with the corrected predicate and its own row set: the session POC value read, the gap geometry that fired (the bar's open, high and low against that POC), the nearest-against comparison of row 2, and the sync attempt that follows - none of which is the closed-session walk's row set. (3) A row records WHICH host acted on the day-close tick, the earlier check or the closed-bar host, read from the latch, so the first-writer-wins rule is observable rather than inferred. PRESERVED, not resolved by this fold: the four open defects - June 5 target-touch management retirement, the RECON57 day-close model-versus-broker close, the day-close price reference and the day-mark pilot window - and the day-mark pilot bound itself IS that fourth defect, measured at EA 10882-10883, EA 11005-11006 and EA 11021-11022, which is why this DAY_CLOSE branch is not gradeable on any June fixture until it closes. No numeric retcode, overload, history call, close reason, hedging or netting posture, or asynchronous setting is asserted by this acceptance; 15.4.6 governs them.

For a static line those reads agree; for a moving line - VWAP, or a POC still developing - they need not, so no no-drift claim is made and the acceptance prints the line value each reader used. P7188:
P7189: And the predicate rows, restated so each resolves to a page term: S1 the prior-candle arm is REMOVED when the retest bar is the confirmation bar and RETAINED when it is the prior bar, on the amended-point sentence that the bar before the retest never judges - **and the retained term is now present in the rule text, which v15 omitted.** S2 and S3, the close-side arm and its reclaim variant, are REMOVED in both forms, and with them the reclaim parameter and the equality counters they measured, including the deferred readers and every save and restore wrapper, all dispositioned in 15.5.4. S4 the doji test, S5 the body direction and its return, S7 the touch return, and S8 the line read are RETAINED. S6 keeps both containment bounds and loses the band. The pre-condition guards - no anchor, no data, no line - are RETAINED unchanged, because none of them judges the prior bar.
P7190:
P7191: **15.5.2 The corrected rule, every variable bound, with one bar convention.** Let `B` be the EVALUATED bar: the pass under evaluation closed it, so its own time is `tEval` and the forming bar begins one period later. The forming bar is named FORMING and is never a retest bar. Let `R` be the RETEST bar, named by SHIFT so that no symbol can serve twice: in the same-bar form `R` IS `B`, and in the general form `R` is the bar BEFORE `B`, that is shift `barShift + 1`, whose time is `tEval` minus one period. The previous wording that made the general form's retest bar `B+1` is withdrawn, because it named the forming bar as the retest bar and a chronological reading would have confirmed on a bar that has not closed. The form is selected by an or over both candidates, stated rather than assumed, and BOTH may hold for one bar, so the acceptance reports which disjuncts fired rather than a single form. Let `L` be the line the predicate itself reads: it takes the anchor INDEX and reads the price out of the POI buffer at the shift it is handed (EA 2351), and that read governs. The float guard is one thousandth of a point, defined twice in the source and named there a threshold-free guard; it is a guard, not a margin, and it is applied to the open side, the touch containment and the hold alike.
P7192:
P7193: CONFIRMED(B) requires, in the same-bar form, that the retest bar IS the evaluated bar, and in the general form that the retest bar is the bar before it; and then either of the two arms below holds. The retest bar's OPEN-side term belongs to the TOUCH arm alone and is written inside that arm, so the BREAK arm is not made unsatisfiable by it; the previous sentence prefix governed both arms by grammar and is withdrawn.
P7194:
P7195: - TOUCH arm, with the open-side term attached here and to no other arm: the retest bar's OPEN is on the setup side of the line, inclusive of equality - long at or above, short at or below; AND R's range contains the line exactly, at or above and at or below, band-free; AND B closes into setup-bias direction; AND B's close holds on the setup side, equality holding and not breaking; AND, in the general form only, R's own body CLOSES AGAINST setup-bias direction. That last term is the retained prior-candle arm at EA 2366-2367 and its polarity is the source's own: the arm passes when `oppCandle` is true, that is when the prior candle closed against the setup direction, and it fails with `A_OPP` when it did not. The previous fold rendered this term as "does not close against", which is the negation of the arm and would have flipped every retracement-shaped general-form setup from confirmed to refused; that wording is withdrawn, and with it the doji it admitted. A flat retest bar fails the RETAINED ARM FIRST: with `c1 == o1` the source's `oppCandle` is false, so EA 2367 returns `A_OPP` before the current-bar doji test at EA 2370-2371 is reached. The earlier fold attributed that failure to `B_BODY`, which tests the current candle and belongs to the same-bar form; the correct general-form attribution is `A_OPP`, corrected here.
P7196: - BREAK arm, with its own retest definition so that the open-side term does not contradict it, and with the operand set pinned rather than asserted: the body convention is the bar's OWN open-close, `body = MathAbs(c0 - o0)` as the source computes it at EA 2370, NOT the open-to-next-open pair the day-close price reference uses at EA 11859-11860; the crossing test is that the body's closing value is strictly beyond the line in setup-bias direction, long `c0 > L` with `o0 <= L` so the bar opened at or through the line, short `c0 < L` with `o0 >= L`. Exact equality is not a break, matching the source's own strictness note at EA 11952-11953. The retest this arm requires is its own: the bar opens at or through the line and closes strictly beyond it, so a break is a close-through that began at or beyond the line. Its POPULATION IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS and the diff class says so: the detector already forces `min(o, cNext) >= L - EPS` on any seeded candidate, so with this arm's `o0 <= L` the arm admits only bars whose open sits within one guard-width of the line, and it differs from the touch arm only in the general form, where the touch arm fails `A_OPP` on the retest bar while this arm carries no retest-direction term. The journal diff class is therefore "general-form close-through with an open at or within one guard-width of the line", not a broad new population. Its shape is the reclaimed-through geometry the earlier fold removed as a confirmation variant, named here as a DIFF CLASS of its own rather than smuggled back in, because the saved predicate has no break arm at all.
P7197:
P7198: What the page may claim about the registered case is bounded by what the spliced rows carry. The retest-detector row for the Daily-POC line gives the line at 160.523 and the 14:35 close at 160.526, and because the detector's body low is `min(o, cNext)` tested against `L - EPS`, that row implies only that the 14:35 OPEN is at or above 160.523. The previous sentence's claim that the open EQUALS the line is withdrawn: no spliced row carries that open, and the chart value stays an operator-side gap at 15.6. Whether the 14:35 close is bullish also depends on its open, which no row proves, so the acceptance prints both opens and both bodies rather than asserting the case. Under the corrected polarity both disjuncts of the form or are true for the registered case - the 14:30 bar's range contains the line, its open is above it, and it closes against LONG bias - so the acceptance reports BOTH disjuncts rather than one fired form. THE LINE IS READ PER SHIFT and the page claims no more than that: the predicate takes the anchor index and reads the price at the evaluated shift (EA 2351), the detector read it at its own shift (EA 2098), the seed stored it at the seed shift (EA 8137). For a static line the three agree; for a moving line they can differ, and the read that governs is the predicate's - the source's own behaviour, retained unchanged. The consequence is an acceptance obligation rather than a page claim: the acceptance prints the line value the detector hit and the line value the predicate compared, so a drift is visible instead of silent.
P7199:
P7200: **15.5.3 Caller census, all thirteen classified plus the mirrors.** EIGHT live decision sites, not two: the holder carve, both contender probes, the displace probes, the carry promote, the prebind, and the S4-to-S5 gate. TELEMETRY: the equality counter probe, the seed probe, and the three profile sites whose suppression effect the source comment records as deleted with prints kept and no state write. The inline mirror and the confirmation-poll body are a third and fourth mirror, and their disposition is CLOSED rather than offered as a choice: BOTH MIGRATE to the corrected terms, and 15.5.7a now carries the mirror-row term this paragraph promised, so each migrated mirror prints a row naming which mirror produced it. The choice is made because a mirror left on the old terms prints rows that flip class against the live path - the poll body carries the band the live path no longer has and has no close-side term at all - while a mirror declared independent would keep printing a class the live path no longer produces. The polarity correction applies to their rendering of the prior-candle term as well, and each mirror's class flips are named in the acceptance as mirror rows rather than left as unexplained diffs.
P7201:
P7202: **15.5.4 Counter and guard dispositions, with every reader named.** Guards retained unchanged. The equality counters are removed with the arm they measured, together with every save and restore wrapper at the call sites, so no wrapper is left restoring a counter that no longer exists. The removal reaches the whole family, because the counters are written at A_OPP, A2_CLOSE_BREAK, B_BODY and C_TOUCH as well as at the equality test itself, and the reader census is: `g_n1_vwapEq` and `g_n1_pocEq` DECLARED at EA 1130-1131, written at EA 2363-2364, EA 8243, EA 8269-8270, EA 8338-8339, EA 8361-8362 and EA 8368-8369, and read at EA 11329, EA 8240, EA 8261-8262, EA 8330-8331 and EA 8352-8353 - EA 1130-1131 are declarations and the earlier fold listed them as reads, which is corrected here; SCOPED TO THE VWAP AND POC COUNTERS ONLY, which is what the removed arm measured: their surv/inv partners - the ONLY members of that declaration block this removal touches, EA 1143-1146, since EA 1139-1142 are the RETAINED entry counters and EA 1147-1148 the RETAINED exit-body counters - DECLARED across EA 1139-1148 and written at EA 2367, EA 2369, EA 2373, EA 2375, EA 2376, EA 8243, EA 8271-8274, EA 8340-8343, EA 8363-8366, EA 8370-8373, and NOT EA 11968, which is struck from that list because it writes the retained `g_n1_exitBodySurv/Inv` pair rather than the VWAP and POC partners and is named below as a retained write instead; read at EA 8263-8266, EA 8332-8335, EA 8354-8357 and, of the EA 11333-11336 pair block, only EA 11335 - the entry wick, entry body and exit-body fields there are RETAINED and their print sites stay. The earlier fold also listed EA 2121 and EA 2143 in this sentence and called the whole set reads; both corrections are made together - EA 2121, EA 2143 and EA 11968 are `+=` WRITES, and the entry wick, entry body and exit body counters they write are RETAINED because they measure the retained arms rather than the removed one. The surviving N1EQUALS and N1PAIR print formats keep the entry wick, entry body and exit body pairs at EA 11333-11336 plus whatever the telemetry sites still print without the removed arm, and lose only the VWAP and POC fields; every removed field is struck from the format rather than left printing a stale zero. The single call site that passed the removed reclaim parameter is EA 8438, the contender probe, which becomes a four-argument call with the parameter defaulted off. F11 STATE COVERAGE, the census the consume placement required: `uj_saAbort` is declared and initialised false at EA 6939, SET at exactly one site, EA 7454, READ at EA 8463 and CLEARED at EA 8475, so a consume placed at that one set site intercepts every defer-set path in the block and no path can reach the apply site carrying a live abort the consume missed. The apply and drop rows at EA 8467 and EA 8473 are therefore retired with the route that could reach them.
P7203:
P7204: **15.5.5 The branch, with ONE ROW SET PER ROUTE, and the stale narrative struck.** The v15 text that graded June 11 as yield is withdrawn, and the correction is made explicitly because June 11's holder IS flip-killed.
P7205:
P7206: - FLIP-KILLED ROUTE - the registered case. Rows emitted, in order: the deferred-abort row at the set site, which prints the SET; the consume row, which clears that same flag at the set site so no deferred abort survives it; one stand-down; the shadow and refused-candidate rows for the holder; then the fresh-seed rows and the ordinary stages. NOT emitted: the apply row and the drop row, both unreachable on this route because the flag the apply site tests was cleared at the set site; the yield row, for the reason 15.5.6 gives; `SIDE1H_WOULDPREEMPT`, which sits at EA 7834 under the `g_state > ST_IDLE` gate at EA 7814 and therefore vanishes once the consume has left the state IDLE; and no false held suppression for the challenger, the SUPPRESSED census being gated the same way at EA 8013-8014. Two corrections the seats forced: the row's own text "abort deferred past evaluation" is right for the SET and wrong for the route, so the row is relabelled as a set row; and the holder's CONFIRMPOLL row is REPLACED by the challenger's, because the shadow poll's IDLE branch at EA 8061 runs after the consume and polls the fresh candidate instead.
P7207: - NON-FLIP-KILLED FAMILY. Rows emitted: the contender probes and the yield row, then the designed path's own named rows. NOT emitted: the consume row or the stand-down.
P7208:
P7209: Mechanism placement, decided here because it is builder authority and no pin is in question: the consume sits where the deferred abort is SET, at EA 7454, which is before the stop-reference block and before the seed-gated block, so the challenger is seeded fresh in the same pass. It does NOT sit at the later apply site, because consuming there would skip the ordinary seed entirely. Placing it at the set site also avoids inheriting the holder's stage-two values: a consume placed after the stop-reference and memo block would carry the holder's stop reference and memo, and the fire-edge guard would then fail on identity rather than on merit. Two corrections the seats forced, both stated because the previous fold asserted the opposite. FIRST, the challenger does NOT carry the stage-two guards: the state is IDLE at that point, so the stop-reference and memo block at EA 7591-7640 is skipped for it, the fire-site memo fallback at EA 10670-10683 covers the memo, and S5 re-derives target and stop at EA 9359 and EA 9383 - nothing is lost, but the claim that the guards ride along is withdrawn. SECOND, the ordering claim is narrowed to what the line order supports: only the holder's own confirm at EA 7449 is evaluated before the set site, so the tie this mechanism relies on is the holder-confirm tie alone. The contender probes at EA 8438-8439 run AFTER the set site, so a flip-killed holder with a confirmed opposite contender no longer yields at EA 8443-8460 - it is consumed and freshly seeded instead. That is a behaviour change on the contender-confirm case and it is named as its own DIFF CLASS; the pin is read as the holder-confirm tie, and the third reason GLM supplied is adopted, because under the corrected predicate the contender probes would otherwise route the registered case through the yield and hand it the holder's stage-two status.
P7210:
P7211: **15.5.6 What the yield actually leaves behind, stated exactly.** Carried from v15 and unchanged: the transfer re-keys the anchor line, direction, price and bar time and zeroes the zone, touch flags, latches and memo; it does NOT reset the state, regime, session, divergence latch, alert-armed flag, or the seed-leg and reseed carriers. A challenger arriving by yield therefore inherits the holder's regime and stage-two status, which is exactly why the flip-killed case is routed through a fresh seed instead. The stage-four zone re-read is asymmetric against a zeroed lower bound and the mechanism does not rely on it in either direction.
P7212:
P7213: **15.5.7 Acceptance.** Registered identity 11 June, New York, USDJPY, LONG, Daily-POC. Required rows: the open, high, low and close of BOTH candidate bars with the line, including both opens, because the open-side and body terms are otherwise unproven; which form fired; each per-term row named on the pass that uses it; the old predicate's failing term named on the flip; the route's own rows from 15.5.5 and the incumbent's disposition rows; the fresh-initialization rows - regime, alignment, mid-term bias - on the flip-killed route; the zone evidence; the gate rows including the divergence proof; the admission, snapshot and signal rows with the entry reference exactly 160.524; the session-use call; the deal recorded separately with the structural offset as class one and any excess latency as class two measured against the FIRST TICK of the bar, never the signal-time spread field; no tolerance, no relabel, no rounding; no 14:45-or-later selection evidence; exactly one accepted take scoped by pair, session, date, anchor and direction. Ordering: the June 11 admission requires the June 5 New York position broker-closed by any Q1 closing leg; if it is open, the guard must block and the block is correct behaviour. Preservation: 5 June New York silence on its observed kill, 9 June never-reseeded, 5 June London 09:45 at 159.948, 3 June 09:10, the seven EURUSD takes, one take per pair, session and day, cross-session independence. The full-journal diff carries SIX named classes - band removal, close-side removal, the retained general-form term, the new break arm, the migrated-mirror class flips, and the contender-confirm route reversal - with zero unexplained flips, and the two added classes are what the mirror migration and the narrowed ordering claim in 15.5.3 and 15.5.5 actually change and every refused-to-confirmed flip mapped to a register row.
P7214:
P7215: **15.5.7a Acceptance additions, printed here for the same reason.** SEVEN ADDITIONS, each from a named seat condition. (1) The June 8 preservation control is restored: the 8 June SHORT must stay refused on its observed `S2SEEDBIAS_KILL`, and the diff must show that refusal unchanged; "5 June New York silence on its observed kill" denotes the Q2-closed 16:15 registered miss, carried as context and neither erased nor reopened. (2) A preservation-gap row is required for the fresh-seed route: the challenger's own absence of `SUPPRESSED action=HELD`, so the flip-killed route cannot be credited with a suppression it never performed. (3) Every row printed on the pass must carry `o0` explicitly, because the open-side and body terms are otherwise unproven from the rows. (4) The contender-confirm tie, if the run does not exercise it, is declared NOT GRADED rather than preserved, so an unexercised tie is never read as a preserved one. (5) Both disjuncts of the form or are reported, and "which form fired" is replaced by "which disjuncts fired", because under the corrected polarity the registered case satisfies both the same-bar and the general form. (6) Two proofs are required before any acceptance passes: the complete once-only `(barTime, phase, candidate identity)` execution census for the counters this fold touches, and a proof that every eviction-bit TOUCH POINT - the declarations at EA 1939 and EA 1940, the day-change clears at EA 1965, EA 1971, EA 8111 and EA 8116, the bit-set writers at EA 9323 and EA 9325, and the blocking reads at EA 1961, EA 1967, EA 8112 and EA 8117, the first two now spliced at EX-33 - cannot let the consumed SHORT identity suppress or evict the new LONG identity. Only `ABORT_DIV_FALLBACK` at EA 9316 reaches the bit-set writers, while the flip abort used on this route is `GoAbort(ABORT_LTF_MISALIGN)`, so the consumed SHORT identity never writes a bit on this path at all; the bit index is `topLine*2 + (isLong ? 0 : 1)`, so a SHORT and a LONG identity on the same line occupy different bits. The earlier fold called all twelve sites writes; only EA 9323 and EA 9325 set bits, four clear, two declare, and four read. The proof runs over the twelve touch points because a read that blocks a seed matters as much as a write that evicts one. (7) A MIRROR-ROW TERM, which the earlier fold promised here and did not write: each migrated mirror - the inline profile at EA 8302-8312 and the confirmation-poll body at EA 2292-2319 - prints a row naming which mirror produced it and which terms it was migrated to, so a mirror class flip is attributed rather than inferred. Without this row the "zero unexplained flips" clause below rests on row-type inference.
P7216:
P7217: ### 15.8 Crosswalk and the relation gate, printed so a seat can re-run it
P7218:
P7219: **Why this section exists.** The V407 seats found that the relation gate lived only in the relay header, so no seat could re-run it, and that the page carried no crosswalk of the conditions it claimed to discharge, so discharge could not be checked one by one. Both are fixed by printing them here. This section is reference material for the reviewer and authorizes nothing.
P7220:
P7221: **The gate, BIDIRECTIONAL this time.** The first version ran one way: for every phrase the fold replaced, a six-word fingerprint was searched across the page and a hit outside the edited line was a missed cell. It caught two carried sections but was blind in three named ways, and all three produced conditions this round. (a) It could not see a sentence nobody edited that still DESCRIBES a corrected thing - a sentence reading "a single value as 15.5.1 S8 now states" while S8 itself had been rewritten to say the line is read per shift. (b) Its promise-resolution part asserted that a promise PHRASE existed rather than that the promise REQUIREMENT existed, so a sentence claiming the acceptance now carries a mirror-row term passed while the term was absent. (c) It had no duplicate check and no splice-coverage check. The three additions, all mechanical: BACKWARD REFERENCE SWEEP - every sentence naming a corrected subsection id or census row is classified POINTER (no restatement) or RESTATEMENT (one), and a restatement outside the fold edit set is a missed cell unless it is adjudicated in writing; REQUIREMENT-RESOLUTION - a promise is satisfied only when the requirement it names exists in the acceptance or the section it points at, asserted by content and not by phrase; SPLICE COVERAGE plus DE-DUPLICATION - every EA line cited by a census on this page must appear in some spliced region, and no builder-prose sentence over 200 characters may appear twice.

P7243: ### 15.6 Provenance and record hygiene
P7244:
P7245: Source, EX5 and run bound separately; linkage unproven until exhibited. Acceptance uses future-run rows only. No RECON57 physical row is cited in acceptance until the segment exists and is re-extracted, and the model-versus-broker divergence stays diagnosable only when a future run surfaces the close-attempt retcodes and deal-resolution rows. The historical "register row 26" citation stays struck; no such row exists. THE REGISTERED BAR OPENS, AS DERIVATIONS AND NOT AS MEASUREMENTS: the confirmation-poll row prints the SHORT holder's `bodyDir=0` with a three-point body and `doji=0`, and the telemetry row gives that bar's close at 160.526, so the 14:35 open derives to 160.523, which is the line - the page therefore states both as DERIVED, with the derivation shown, and the acceptance still PRINTS both opens rather than relying on them. Two consequences follow and are stated rather than discovered later: the open-side term is load-bearing at equality for this case, and the break arm's own population includes the registered bar on the same derivation. The June 11 chart values remain an operator-side gap that blocks nothing. SUPERSEDED PHRASING IN CARRIED HISTORY, recorded so the relation check has an owner: two lines in the carried sections - the V396 binding-requirements paragraph and the function-return-census requirement - still say "g_evictBits writes" where 15.5.7a now classifies twelve touch points as two declarations, four clears, two bit-set writers and four reads. Those lines are left verbatim because carried history is not rewritten; the classification that GOVERNS is the one in 15.5.7a, and the phrase "writes" in the earlier sections is read there as "touch points".

P7262:
P7263: The relation gate banked at v19 was bypassed in a NEW way: it swept the PHRASES a fold replaced but not the live premises of the OLD disposition, so a disposition changed in one place stayed live in others. The step added now and banked is **DISPOSITION SWEEP**, three mechanical parts: (a) a disposition changed in one place is searched across the page for live premises of the old disposition, and each is struck or re-scoped; (b) a crosswalk row is tested against the REQUIREMENT it points at, not against the phrase; (c) a header count is tested against its own list. It is counted with COUNT-VS-ENUMERATION and CROSSWALK-COVERAGE, both banked this round.

P7316: ### 16.5 15.3.3, one latch scope (Q1 condition 11)
P7317:

The three renderings the seats found are one rule read three ways, and two are corrected here: the latch key is (instance, evaluated bar) and its effect is to stand the closed-bar host down for the WHOLE evaluation across all five legs. The phrase "stands down from the day-close leg" is STRUCK as a scope error and reads "stands down from the whole evaluation"; the phrase "once-per-instance latch" is STRUCK and reads "per-instance, per-evaluated-bar latch". No third scope survives.

P7322: - **15.5.1 header count (Q3-19).** "The eighteen predicate rows and the three added rows" reads "The nineteen predicate rows and the three added rows": the table is S1-S10, G1 and P1-P8, nineteen non-T rows, corrected by COUNT-VS-ENUMERATION.
P7323: - **Reader census adds EA 10731 (Q1-12).** `uj_tradeSeqNext` is read at EA 10700 AND at EA 10731 (`g_mtrade.uj_tradeSeq = uj_tradeSeqNext; uj_tradeSeqNext++`), so "read at exactly one place" is withdrawn; the global read list is EA 10700 and EA 10731.
P7324: - **Reader census adds EA 8240 (Q3-20).** EA 8240 reads all six counters `g_n1_vwapEq, g_n1_pocEq, g_n1_vwapInv, g_n1_pocInv, g_n1_vwapSurv, g_n1_pocSurv`, the Inv/Surv partners included, so it belongs on both the Eq list and the Surv/Inv list.
P7325: - **The "exactly one" set site (Q3-21) is shown, not asserted.** A full-function grep from EA 6939: the four variables are DECLARED at EA 6939-6942 (`uj_saAbort`, `uj_saA`, `uj_saD`, `uj_saT`), **SET at exactly EA 7454** (all four in one statement), READ at EA 8465, and CLEARED at EA 8475 (`uj_saAbort = false`). No other assignment exists in the span, so the set site is EA 7454 alone; EX-31 splices that line.
P7326: - **Challenger rows (Q3-22).** The acceptance adds the challenger's own `UJSBTELEM` row at EA 8442 beside the CONFIRMPOLL replacement (different rows), and "left the state IDLE" reads "**enters** IDLE" - the consume ENTERS IDLE at the set site, it does not leave it.

The page now states one derived and one printed.

The class count is now seven.
P7329: - **15.4.4 read order (Q1-13).** The earlier check's guard at EA 12278 precedes pool refresh (EA 12281), working-set load (EA 12282) and memo clear (EA 12285); `MtNearestTpTarget` reads `uj_pool` (EA 11618, EA 11661). The order is stated in that sequence.
P7330: - **SYNC_FAILED at row 2 (Q1-14).** Row 2 compares against "booked and pending"; a SYNC_FAILED revision is NOT pending and does NOT count, so it cannot cancel a newer revision.

P7371: ### 17.0 What this fold is
P7372:
P7373: V409 graded Q1 AMEND and Q3 CONDITIONAL CONFIRM (Luna CONFIRM, GLM CONFIRM, Sonnet DISCREPANCY; no OBJECT, no NO). The grade is `BUILDER_RESULT_V409-GRADE.md`; the replies are filed whole under `V409-UJ-EXEC-26`. Twenty conditions came back, all page-text - except one item this fold reverses on the operator's own ruling. The operator then answered the two batched questions; his answer is `BUILDER_DECISION_GAPPEDPOC-PIN117_2026-10-03.md` and it governs. **Section 17 is the controlling v21 scope; where it names a cell it supersedes that cell's earlier wording, and it supersedes Section 16.2 in full.** No source edit, build, test, tester run, key request, live action, commit or push is authorized. SRJ stays alert-only.
P7374:
P7375: ### 17.1 The operator's ruling, and the reversal it forces
P7376:
P7377: His words: "POC = point of control from the anchored volume profile, B." and "the newly closed session as a valid TP target is also dynamic because it moved from the static original TP, revised to that because it is closer."
P7378:
P7379: - **"gapped POC" means the point of control from the anchored volume profile: ALL SIX of its POC lines** - FOMC-POC, Yearly-POC, Quarterly-POC, Monthly-POC, Weekly-POC and Daily-POC - each of which can be gapped away from. The gap is NOT limited to a session POC; the operator's own words are "it's all of them 6", so the detector scans the whole POC universe of the anchored volume profile, not one line and not a subset. The six POC lines are the ones enumerated at EA 93-104 (EX-34).
P7380: - **THE GAPPED-POC DETECTOR IS SOURCED.** It reads the existing anchored-volume-profile POC lines already in the POI universe at EA 93-104, which EX-34 splices. The "UNSOURCED and NOT GRADED" declaration and **Section 16.2's five strikes are WITHDRAWN**; the gapped-POC branch is **IN SCOPE and gradeable**.
P7381: - **Why 16.2 was wrong:** it rested on a premise the operator never stated - that the gapped POC must be a *session* POC - and found no session POC in the twelve-line universe. The correct reading is a *profile* POC line, and those lines are present. The declaration is reversed; the fold records that the error was mine, not the seats'.
P7382: - The predicate geometry the page already stated stands (target AHEAD of price); only the SOURCE was in dispute and is now decided.
P7383: - The second half of pin 32 is confirmed: the newly-closed session's H/L is also a valid dynamic TP target, revised from the static original TP because it is nearer.
P7384:
P7385: ### 17.2 The gapped-POC detector, restored as sourced (this supersedes 16.2)
P7386:
P7387: - 15.4.1's closing sentence "Its producer is the revision-detection writer named in 15.4.2." is **RESTORED and corrected**: the detector's source is the anchored-volume-profile POC lines in the POI universe, and the producer is the POI POC computation that fills them.
P7388: - 15.4.1's fragment "its detection predicate and its producer are now named" is **RESTORED** (V409 condition 2 resolved by the ruling - the fragment is correct, not stale).
P7389: - 15.4.2's "the gapped POC needed a predicate of its own rather than the recompute" is **RESTORED**.
P7390: - 15.4.3 row 2's event is **RESTORED** to "Eligible nearer closed-session target or gapped POC at or after the fill".
P7391: - 15.4.7 addition (2)'s gapped-POC fixture is **RESTORED** and gradeable, with the row set already named there (the session-POC value read becomes the POC-line value read, the gap geometry, the nearest-against comparison, the sync attempt).
P7392: - 15.7's Q1 line is **RESTORED** to "the gapped-POC predicate and its producer named", and "both detectors listed" is likewise restored.
P7393: - 16.7 rows 1-4 are **re-pointed**: those four conditions are discharged by this restoration, not by the strikes.
P7394: - **The predicate, re-derived against the existing POC lines:** a gapped POC is an anchored-volume-profile POC line that price has gapped THROUGH and now sits beyond, so it remains a target AHEAD of price - for a LONG the POC line lies at or ABOVE the current bar's HIGH with that bar's open at or BELOW it, and for a SHORT the mirror. The geometry is the source's own reclaimed-through shape at EA 2368 applied to a revision target, not to a confirmation.
